  .hidden {
    display: none;

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">MoneyTaker: in pursuit of the invisible</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Group-IB has uncovered a hacker group attacking banks in the USA and Russia</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">December 11, 2017 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-20.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-20.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-20.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Hacker group</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Incident Response</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">MoneyTaker</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Russia</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">USA</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>In less than two years, this group has conducted over 20 successful attacks on financial institutions and legal firms in the USA, UK and Russia. The group has primarily been targeting card processing systems, including the AWS CBR (Russian Interbank System) and purportedly SWIFT (US). Given the wide usage of STAR in LATAM, financial institutions in LATAM could have particular exposure to a potential interest from the MoneyTaker group. <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/research-hub/money-taker/"><strong>Get the report</strong></a></p>
<p>Although the group has been successful at targeting a number of banks in different countries, to date, they have gone unreported. In addition to banks, the MoneyTaker group has attacked law firms and also financial software vendors. In total, Group-IB has confirmed 20 companies as MoneyTaker victims, with 16 attacks on US organizations, 3 attacks on Russian banks and 1 in the UK.</p>
<p>By constantly changing their tools and tactics to bypass antivirus and traditional security solutions and most importantly carefully eliminating their traces after completing their operations, the group has largely gone unnoticed.</p>
</div>
                                                            <div class="experts--type1 mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48 mt-24 mt-lg-32 mt-xl-48">
                                    <div class="experts__quot">
                                        <div class="experts__quot-text">
                                            <div class="experts__quot-icon"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/quot.svg" alt=""></noscript></div>
                                            <div class="title-h5">"MoneyTaker uses publicly available tools, which makes the attribution and investigation process a non-trivial exercise. In addition, incidents occur in different regions worldwide and at least one of the US Banks targeted had documents successfully exfiltrated from their networks, twice. Group-IB specialists expect new thefts in the near future and in order to reduce this risk, Group-IB would like to contribute our report identifying hacker tools, techniques as well as indicators of compromise we attribute to MoneyTaker operations".</div>
                                        </div>
                                        <div class="experts__quot-author">
                                            <div class="author__info">
                                                                                                                                                    <div class="author__about">
                                                        <div class="author__description body2">Dmitry Volkov, CEO of Group-IB</div>
                                                    </div>
                                                                                            </div>
                                        </div>
                                    </div>
                                </div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>MoneyTaker attacks: past and future</h2>
<p>The first attack in the US that Group-IB attributes to this group was conducted in the spring of 2016: money was stolen from the bank by gaining access to First Data’s &#8220;STAR&#8221; network operator portal. Since that time, the group attacked companies in California, Utah, Oklahoma, Colorado, Illinois, Missouri, South Carolina, North Carolina, Virginia and Florida.</p>
<p>In 2016, Group-IB identified 10 attacks conducted by <strong>MoneyTaker</strong>; 6 attacks on <strong>banks in the US</strong>, 1 attack on <strong>a US service provider</strong>, 1 attack on <strong>a bank in the UK</strong> and 2 <strong>attacks on Russian banks</strong>. Only one incident involving a Russian bank was promptly identified and prevented that is known to Group-IB.</p>
<p>In 2017, the number of attacks has remained the same with 8 US banks, 1 law firm and 1 bank in Russia being targeted. The geography, however, has narrowed to only the USA and Russia.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-27776 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201185%201015'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="moneytaker geography" width="1185" height="1015" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4-2.png"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-27776 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-4-2.png" alt="moneytaker geography" width="1185" height="1015"></noscript></picture></p>
<h2>Connections between incidents</h2>
<p>Using the Group-IB <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/products/threat-intelligence/">Threat Intelligence</a> system, Group-IB researchers have discovered connections between all 20 incidents throughout 2016 and 2017. Connections were identified not only in the tools used, but also the distributed infrastructure, one-time-use components in the attack toolkit of the group and specific withdrawal schemes – using unique accounts for each transaction. Another distinct feature of this group is that they stick around after the event, continuing to spy on a number of impacted banks and sending corporate emails and other documents to Yandex and Mail.ru free email services in the <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="93f5fae1e0e7bdfff2e0e7d3eaf2fdf7f6ebbdf0fcfe">[email&#160;protected]</a> format.</p>
<p>Important findings that enabled Group-IB to discover the links between crimes include privilege escalation tools compiled based on codes presented at the Russian cybersecurity conference ZeroNights 2016. Also, in some incidents, hackers used the infamous Citadel and Kronos banking Trojans. The latter was used to deliver Point-of-Sale (POS) malware dubbed ScanPOS.</p>
<div id="attachment_27775" style="width: 873px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-3-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-27775" decoding="async" class="wp-image-27775 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20863%20596'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Connections between MoneyTaker incidents" width="863" height="596" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-3-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-27775" decoding="async" class="wp-image-27775 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-3-1.png" alt="Connections between MoneyTaker incidents" width="863" height="596"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-27775" class="wp-caption-text">Connections between incidents</p></div>
<h2>MoneyTaker: arsenal for attacks</h2>
<p>Group-IB reports that <strong>MoneyTaker uses both borrowed and their own self-written tools</strong>. For example, to spy on bank operators they developed an application with &#8216;screenshot&#8217; and &#8216;keylogger&#8217; capabilities. This program is designed to capture keystrokes, take screenshots of the user&#8217;s desktop and get contents from the clipboard. The application is compiled in Delphi and contains 5 timers: functions of the application (such as taking screenshots, capturing keystrokes, disabling itself) are executed once the timer triggers. To circumvent antivirus and automated sample analysis, hackers again used &#8216;security measures&#8217;: they implemented the anti-emulation function in the timer code.</p>
<p>In an attack on a Russian bank through the AWS CBR, hackers used a tool called MoneyTaker v5.0, which the group has been named after. Each component of this modular program performs a certain action: searches for payment orders and modifies them, replaces original payment details with fraudulent ones, and then erases traces. The success of replacement is due to the fact that at this stage the payment order has not yet been signed, which will occur after payment details are replaced. In addition to hiding the tracks, the concealment module again substitutes the fraudulent payment details in a debit advice after the transaction back with the original ones. This means that the payment order is sent and accepted for execution with the fraudulent payment details, and the responses come as if the payment details were the initial ones. This gives cybercriminals extra time to mule funds before the theft is detected.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="50%;50%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="50%">Created tools</th>
<th width="50%">Borrowed tools</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="50%">MoneyTaker 5.0 &#8211; malicious program for auto replacement of payment data in AWS CBR</td>
<td width="50%">Metasploit и PowerShell Empire</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="50%">‘Screenshotter’ and ‘keylogger’ to conduct espionage and capture keystrokes</td>
<td width="50%">Privilege escalation tools, whose code were demonstrated as a Proof of Concept at ZeroNights cybersecurity conference in Moscow in 2016. More data provided later in this report</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="50%">Moneytaker ‘Auto-replacement’ program to substitute payment details in the interbank transfer system</td>
<td width="50%">Citadel and Kronos Banking Trojans. The latter one was used to deliver a Point-of-Sale (POS) malware dubbed ScanPOS</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Leaving no trace behind</h2>
<p>To conduct targeted attacks, <strong>MoneyTaker use a distributed infrastructure that is difficult to track</strong>. A unique feature of the infrastructure is a persistence server, which delivers payloads only to victims with an IP addresses in MoneyTaker&#8217;s whitelist.</p>
<p>To control the full operation, MoneyTaker uses a Pentest framework Server. On it, the hackers install a legitimate tool for penetration testing – <strong>Metasploit</strong>. After successfully infecting one of the computers and gaining initial access to the system, the attackers perform reconnaissance of the local network in order to gain domain administrator privileges and eventually consolidate control over the network. Hackers use Metasploit to conduct all these activities: network reconnaissance, search for vulnerable applications, exploit vulnerabilities, escalate systems privileges, and collect information.</p>
<p>The group uses &#8216;fileless&#8217; malware only existing in RAM and is destroyed after reboot. To ensure persistence in the system MoneyTaker relies on PowerShell and VBS scripts &#8211; they are both difficult to detect by antivirus and easy to modify. In some cases, they have made changes to source code &#8216;on the fly&#8217; – during the attack.</p>
<p>After successful infection, they carefully erase malware traces. However, when investigating an incident in Russia, we managed to discover the initial point of compromise: hackers penetrated the bank&#8217;s internal network by gaining access to the home computer of the bank&#8217;s system administrator.</p>
<p>In addition, <strong>to protect C&amp;C communications from being detected by security teams, MoneyTaker employs SSL certificates generated using names of well-known brands</strong>: Bank of America, Federal Reserve Bank, Microsoft, Yahoo, etc.), instead of filling the fields out randomly. In the US, they used the LogMeIn Hamachi solution for remote access.</p>
<h2>Attacks on card processing</h2>
<p>The first attack on card processing that Group-IB specialists attribute to this group was conducted in May 2016. Having gained access to the bank network, the attackers compromised the workstation of First Data&#8217;s STAR network portal operators, making the changes required and withdrawing the money. In January 2017, the attack was repeated in another bank.</p>
<p>The scheme is extremely simple. <strong>After taking control over the bank&#8217;s network, the attackers checked if they could connect to the card processing system.</strong> Following this, they legally opened or bought cards of the bank whose IT system they had hacked. Money mules – criminals who withdraw money from ATMs – with previously activated cards went abroad and waited for the operation to begin. After getting into the card processing system, the attackers removed or increased cash withdrawal limits for the cards held by the mules. They removed overdraft limits, which made it possible to overdraw even with debit cards. Using these cards, the mules withdrew cash from ATMs, one by one. The average loss caused by one attack was about $500,000 USD.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="promo-block"><h2 class="title-h3 mb-xs-16 mb-sm-16 mb-24">MoneyTaker: Revealed After 1.5 Years of Silent Operations</h2><p>Explore how this group managed to hide their traces while conducting 20+ attacks on banks and financial services companies in the USA, UK and Russia.</p><div class="buttons-wrapper mt-md-48 mt-sm-32 mt-xs-24"><a href="https://www.group-ib.com/resources/research-hub/money-taker/" class="button button--filled button--md "><span class="effect slide-up"><span class="effect__label">View report</span><span class="effect__label" aria-hidden="true">View report</span></span></a></div></div></div>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({

            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,
                slidesPerGroup: 2,
            },
        },
    });

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
